madam president, we need more leadership and more coordination and to continue to work with the scientific evidence to give an effective response to this emergency that is testing everything that europe stands for. our first objective is to contain the spread of the virus to protect the population, as asian countries are already doing. our second objective is to provide solutions to the health, social and economic impact. in the health sector, we must give priority to protecting the most vulnerable population: the elderly, the chronically ill and other sick people. we also need to protect all health and safety personnel, because they have more potential risk. we must strengthen health systems with financial resources to ensure their staff and emergency, urgent, primary and home care services, and also to continue to care for patients with other diseases. we must also set up centralised purchases of protective, diagnostic and treatment equipment. the coronavirus is not stopped by alarmism or fear, but by intelligence, unity and responsibility. to avoid an economic depression with devastating consequences for employment, the people's party has therefore put forward a contingency plan with economic and conciliatory measures to help families, workers and the economic sectors most affected, such as the services sector, tourism, hotels, industry, the self-employed and smes. we call on the spanish government, the council meeting this afternoon and the commission to make use of all the financial resources available to alleviate the negative effects on the economy and to help strengthen health and communication systems.
madam president, it's funny how sometimes the smallest enemies are the ones that reveal our greatest weaknesses. we have seen how, without a strong state, without a quality public system, without universal public health systems, control and prevention are much more complicated. yesterday we saw the stock markets plummet; we heard again about the risk premium; we heard again about the oil price war. in short, a market hysteria at anything that affects the richest. i remind you that some of the countries we have not yet recovered from the 2008 scam. we therefore believe that now is the time to be sensible, to put resources in the general interest, to avoid the prescriptions of those who are obscenely taking advantage of this crisis to propose more prescriptions based on cuts and, above all, to take advantage of the debate on the european budget to answer a big question: do we want more cuts or are we going to bet on public services to be stronger in the face of possible crises?
madam speaker, the spread of the coronavirus is a unique public health challenge. the epidemic has not been confined to one area but has spread rapidly worldwide, and the fact that scientists and doctors still have no answers to most of the questions that the public is asking has contributed to the spread of panic. the union should therefore be able to provide additional support to the competent national authorities in order to ensure that the measures taken to control the spread of the disease are effective and proportionate. some member states currently do not allow sanitary ware to be taken out of their borders, so companies with union-wide central warehouses in those member states cannot ensure the distribution of sanitary ware to the rest of europe. the european institutions must protect the rights and interests of all union citizens and put an end to such bad practices.
madam president, the european union must be much more than a political and economic union; it must protect its citizens and look after their welfare. so it has to be decided in the fight against covid-19. first, greater coordination of efforts to contain the spread of the virus is required, and second, much more investment in scientific research by encouraging cooperation between centres of excellence in the field. the imi public-private partnership must show more action and more results, only then will europe be able to contribute to the international effort to develop vaccines and therapeutics against the virus. the figures so far presented by the commission in this area are laughable. thirdly, an articulated response to support business and the economy. the side effects of this public health problem are already evident in many sectors. the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the protection of workers' rights and the right to freedom of movement. the two main elements of this response must be speed and the least possible bureaucracy. we must prevent a temporary crisis from causing irreparable damage to people, the economy and society.
it is indeed necessary, madam president, to make a realistic assessment of the threat posed by the coronavirus to the european union and its possible consequences for human health, the quality of life of people and our economy, and therefore the european union must develop adequate measures in solidarity and coordination to control this epidemic. and you can't be in here. and now, i want to thank all of you from the bottom of my heart for your dedicated medical staff, because only by working together can we defeat this rogue virus. there is a need to coordinate information and prevention measures effectively, to allocate the necessary funds from the european union budget to help member states to support research in order to develop the necessary vaccine as quickly as possible. the uncontrolled spread of the coronavirus is a concern as it threatens to lead to shortages of medicines, as the production and supply chain of medicines is severely affected. here too, the european union must take coordinated action to ensure the uninterrupted supply of medicines to hospitals and patients. it also concerns the european parliament. the european parliament must certainly continue to put in place preventive measures to help protect the people who work here in parliament, including remote working, more flexible organisation of plenary sessions and committee meetings. and it's hard to expect that the threat of an epidemic is short-lived, so you should have an action plan for at least three months.
madam president, we are faced with a crisis which confronts us with a very grave responsibility. we have a health problem, but we also have an economic problem which could jeopardise the future of europe. we need to show that all the european institutions have learned from the mistakes of the past, from how we faced the great crisis of 2008, and that we are capable of giving agile, decisive, firm, coordinated, forceful, necessary responses, and of taking the necessary measures - temporary and structural - that allow our citizens to face the problem and the crisis with hope. we need to convey that we are able to coordinate health responses to the challenges we are going to face, that we are facing, but that we are also able to give responses to our small entrepreneurs, our self-employed, our companies, with one goal: to ensure that this temporary situation does not cause a structural recession. we must not be ashamed of europe, we must appreciate that more europe is needed now than ever before.
madam president, as the number of people affected and killed and the turmoil increase, we must call for responsibility and calm. to responsibility in the effort to not infect ourselves to not infect and to stop the spread of the virus and to calm because panic can cause more damage than the virus itself. we must learn from our vulnerability, which is abysmal in europe. governments are overwhelmed imagine this with a more lethal virus or with biological weapons: an attack. there is no coordination, no plans. a lot of improvisation and contradictions and breakdowns in solidarity. now we can see the difference between a real crisis and alarmist, climate or other fables, as false as they are self-interested. business cannot overshadow security and defense criteria. they cannot be far away and in the hands of third-party productions of eventual urgent need. we need secure borders and national cohesion for united and united reaction and, in addition, coordination. the coronavirus reminds us of basic survival lessons that long peace and well-being had made us forget.
madam president, in the face of the coronavirus: science, prudence and evidence. we cannot allow ourselves to be carried away by irrationality, or by false recipes, or by misinformation. it is essential to coordinate in europe and to share knowledge between member states, because no frontiers are immune. science, prudence, coordination and investment in public and universal health. it is unpleasant to see those who have done so much damage with abusive cuts now lamenting the effects of those same health cuts. it is the tragedy of having taken health as a commodity and not as a right. in addition, now, at this time, i would also like to remember the sanitarians, the ones who are fighting in the sanitary trenches every day against the virus and are putting the health of their fellow citizens before their own. that is an example we must take across europe. strengthening public health should be the lesson we draw from this crisis.
madam president, i agree with what many members have said, including the chairman of my group and the spokeswoman for my group on this subject, but i would like to begin by making an institutional reflection on the constitutional dimension of the negotiation of the multiannual financial framework and the functioning of the european council. we are in a situation where four governments have tried to impose their will on the rest of the european council and have done so, in part also, with a certain connivance on the part of the president of the european council and the president of the commission, who have accepted their blackmail. and what this shows is that the unanimity rule continues to paralyse europe and, in some cases, to impose or facilitate the imposition of the will of a minority of states on the union as a whole. this is unacceptable and we must therefore update the functioning of our institutions, take advantage of the gateways in the lisbon treaty and also propose the reform of the treaty on the functioning of the european union.
in writing. we are at a key moment for the future of europe, when the next european budget will be decided, which will have to face the great challenges facing the eu: the digital transformation, the fight against climate change and migration. the uk's departure has left a hole in the budgets which cannot be reduced to cuts in key items, in order to achieve a strong europe that leaves no one behind. in spain, these cuts could have a major impact, especially in rural areas, as the common agricultural policy would suffer a 14% cut and regional development a 12.7% cut. in other words, they will directly affect our farmers and smes. to get the budget the right size, a reform of eu revenue is urgently needed. the new own resources are an opportunity to combat tax evasion by large companies and to make the big polluters pay for their environmental damage.
mr president, the situation that has developed this week on the greek-turkish border is a violation of the human rights of immigrants and a violation of the democratic values that are supposed to prevail in the european union. this is the result of years of inaction on the part of the european institutions and the member states. it is the result of shameful agreements with third countries, which are paid to take on in a cruel and inhuman way what europe should have taken on in a solidarity and human way. a crisis in the management of asylum in europe has been treated as a threat to the european way of life. this misconception gives rise to reactionary and neo-fascist movements. in lesbos, the violence and impunity with which the far right acts against refugees, journalists and ngos is alarming. if the commission and the council want to find a common response to the current migratory situation, i would ask them to put aside the hypocrisy.
mr president, i would also like to thank the workers of this parliament for allowing us to have this sitting. as we debate this today, thousands of europeans are struggling between life and death in collapsed hospitals, lacking basic supplies, with overcrowded professionals risking their lives to fight this terrible disease. as we debate this, millions of europeans are confined to their homes, fearing for their lives, for the lives of their loved ones, afraid of losing those they love most and of never being able to see them again or say goodbye to them. and as we debate here, hundreds and thousands of businesses, small and medium-sized businesses, self-employed businesses are debating whether they're going to survive or whether they're going to have to close and lay off their workers. it's a terrible situation. but even in these dark moments, we see how, as we debate here, our citizens demonstrate the great values that shape our societies: generosity, commitment and solidarity. and it is this context of commitment, generosity and solidarity that must guide our decisions as legislators. we are at that moment, at that grave moment, at that great moment of living up to where our societies have brought us. we have no vaccines for our citizens or to prevent or mitigate the disease, just as we have no vaccines to prevent infection in our economy. but there is still time to adopt measures to mitigate its effects and to prevent it from destroying the healthy fabric of our economy, destroying businesses and jobs. the measures taken by the commission are in the right direction. we are making decisions that will help our health systems, and we have enabled mechanisms that will enable our countries to cope financially with the crisis. but it's not enough, we have to go further. we have to be brave, because the challenge we face is whether the recession is going to turn into a depression, and this forces us to make the brave and firm decisions that our citizens need. i've been hearing a word these days, a concept that was coined in the 2008 recession: "moral hazard". the moral hazard we have and face is if we are to carry on our backs and on our conscience the death of our citizens. the moral hazard of letting thousands of businesses die, the moral hazard of letting millions of our fellow citizens lose their jobs. that is the real moral hazard we face and that we have to address. in conclusion, i think we must send a clear message: we will do everything possible and impossible to prevent this from happening. because if we don't, if we are not able to prove with facts that our citizens are not alone and that in this crisis no one is going to be left behind, this virus will not only threaten our lives, our jobs, our future and our hopes, but it will have lethally infected our democracy. and we can't afford that. that's not gonna happen. we have found that divided we are weaker, alone we are weaker and together we are stronger. we must do what is necessary. we must do it for our citizens. that will be our commitment.
in writing. the eu is taking decisive action to mitigate the socioeconomic impact on transport and mobility. the aviation sector is suffering hard from the covid-19 crisis. the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the use of the term 'commercial vehicle' to cover vehicles with a maximum mass of less than 100 tonnes. i would also like to acknowledge the good coordination between the commission and the member states in the establishment of the green corridors. these crossborder highways will ease traffic and ensure the transport of essential goods such as food, masks and medical supplies between eu countries. green corridors will also ensure that our carriers travel in complete safety. the commission has heard from governments, such as spain, that these corridors should have rest areas and basic food, welfare and hygiene facilities for the hauliers. i would like to take this opportunity to thank all those who have been so generous in offering food and drink free of charge to our hauliers along the routes, as they have done in my region, aragon. solidarity is the key to overcoming this crisis.
in writing. the coronavirus is a global crisis. today, we are the continent with the most infected people and the highest number of deaths. the professionalism of healthcare personnel, the security forces and bodies, all the workers whose work allows us to cope with our day to day in the best possible way, as well as the exemplary behavior of all citizens, are, without a doubt, our best weapon to win against covid-19. however, the european citizens are asking for a coordinated, united and united response from the eu, and we cannot let them down. the 37 billion euros we have approved in parliament shows that we are moving in the right direction, but the countries most affected need more: it is urgent to mobilise the european stability mechanism (esm) to help state governments. the social democrats also call for a common european investment marshall plan, which would include the issuing of 'crown bonds', the introduction of european unemployment insurance or the creation of a temporary european minimum income for workers, the selfemployed and smes. no one can be left unprotected in the face of this crisis. we must act now and we must do so in unity, solidarity and coordination. only then will we be able to meet this challenge successfully.
in writing. the covid-19 crisis reveals the limits of our societies and socioeconomic systems, where excessive economic interests, concentration of wealth, unsustainable production systems and the shrinking role of the government to a minimum, have relegated many citizens and territories. this crisis is a wake-up call and an opportunity to build global resilience and a more cohesive society towards sustainable well-being where we all act together in the face of the challenge of citizen protection and social peace and essential social services. the socialists therefore call for an immediate coordinated european response, the concentration of all eu r & d investment to find a vaccine, the stockpiling of essential medical supplies and the provision of diagnostic tests for the most affected regions. we also call for the issuing of 'crown bonds' by the european central bank, the activation of the european stability mechanism, a temporary european minimum income scheme for low-income workers and families and the creation of a guarantee to deal with potential mass unemployment. we need a european strategy for social and economic recovery and reconstruction, a marshall plan to preserve the well-being achieved alongside a transition to a climate-neutral economy.
in writing. the world, europe and, in particular, some member states, are facing an exceptional global health emergency which we will only be able to overcome if we work together, in coordination and cooperation, with the greatest possible efficiency and solidarity. this crisis is having a high human cost for society as a whole, but the professionalism of health workers, security forces, transport workers, food distribution workers, essential services, rural workers and others is certainly to be appreciated. the eu is suffering from the spread of contagion and all governments are, to a greater or lesser extent, taking containment measures, but the success of which is subject to coordination at european level. more than ever, our future depends on our joint action. citizens expect a coordinated community response that strengthens our health systems and addresses urgent material needs; that ensures the distribution of essential goods; and, above all, that comes to the rescue to mitigate the negative effects of the extraordinary measures that are being taken by most governments to contain the virus and that already negatively affect our economies.
in writing. the world is facing an exceptional global health emergency that we can only overcome if we work together. the first steps have already been taken, but we still need more ambitious measures to support national efforts and ensure financial solidarity. that is why, in line with the initiative launched by the spanish prime minister, we european socialists have asked the european leaders to adopt a plan with 25 urgent measures to combat this social and economic crisis. among them, we call for a new marshall plan for common european investment, the activation of the european stability mechanism with a specific credit line, the launch of a european unemployment insurance combined with a minimum income, a temporary european employment guarantee to prevent mass unemployment and a loan for first homeowners, a specific scheme to protect tenants against evictions, measures to ensure the distribution of essential goods and medical equipment and better coordination and exchange of information between member states on border closures and restrictions. because only through unity, solidarity and defence of the welfare state will we successfully get out of this crisis.
in writing. citizens expect a response from the union to meet this challenge. we need more ambitious measures, particularly in the area of fiscal policy, to support national efforts and to ensure financial solidarity. the european socialists, in line with the initiative of president pedro sánchez, have asked the european leaders to approve a plan with 25 urgent measures to leave no one behind, including: a new marshall plan for common european investment which envisages the issuance of eurobonds and 'crown bonds'; the activation of the european stability mechanism without additional macroeconomic conditions; european unemployment insurance, combined with a minimum income; a temporary european employment guarantee to tackle mass unemployment; or a loan guarantee for first homeowners to prevent mortgage defaults and a specific scheme to protect tenants against evictions. we european socialists want to ensure that no worker, no sector is left alone and that we get out of this 'economic and productive hibernation' without losing businesses and human capital along the way. only from unity and from solidarity and from the defence of the welfare state will we successfully face this crisis.
in writing. in difficult times, it is important to keep our goals clear and to take important decisions that protect the interests of our people and help the european union emerge from this crisis more economically. i want to focus on three issues that are important to keep in mind in order to better cope with the covid-19 pandemic. first, we need to understand that in fighting a pandemic, we have to start at the root. this means that we must be specific in our approach to the people's republic of china and make clear that china, as the leading global economic power, must not allow practices and outdated understandings to prevail, resulting in the trade in open markets of animal species that spread viral diseases. this cannot be tolerated. secondly, the european union's fundamental values - the free movement of services, goods, labour and capital - must be safeguarded even in times of crisis. the most important thing is to ensure that green corridors are in place from the start of any crisis. it's very closely tied to our security. thirdly, we must do everything we can to ensure that the support packages aimed at helping businesses, and in particular smes, are adequate, as they are the ones most affected by the crisis. we also need to ensure that the availability of subsidies and resources for our farmers is not compromised. the european parliament must find ways of continuing to work in such a way that our decisions and actions are timely and that the interests of our people are protected.
in writing. three months after who began warning about covid-19, we gather in this special urgency plenary. spain is in mourning over the immense tragedy that covid-19 brings, whose dimensions are a consequence of the catastrophic management of the government. for ideological reasons, he hid the danger for weeks, making no preparations. he has been criminally negligent and will be held accountable. the government ignored who's january guidelines on the virus's 'international public emergency'. the socialist-communist government put the propaganda interests of the feminist demonstrations of 8 march ahead of the safety, health and life of its fellow citizens. this house too was dragged along by its ideological interests, with greta appearing amid cheers on 4 march, eclipsing all the prevention measures. while the consensus celebrated its ideological orgy here and in spain, donald trump, orbán, poland and my party, vox, were branded as xenophobes for wanting to close borders to the virus, a measure that was later imposed by all the governments of the world. vox will demand that lessons be learned from both the spanish and european governments in order to rebuild a realistic agenda in spain and the eu that meets the needs of our compatriots.
in writing. – i deem it important that we act against any attacks on lgbti rights or women’s rights, also when these attacks are covered by measures given to covid-19 pandemic. access to legal abortion and sexual education must be maintained, also during a sanitary crisis! we, socialists have a clear line on fighting for lgbti rights and women’s rights, which we will not drop just because we are currently undergoing an exceptional sanitary crisis. in this sense, i strongly condemn the attempts by the hungarian government to backtrack on trans- and intersex persons’ rights and the attempts of the polish government to further criminalise abortion care, stigmatise hiv positive people and undermine young people’s access to sexual education! i also underline that member states must always follow eu rules and therefore internal border controls must be based on the existing legal provisions and must remain exceptional, limited in time, justified, proportionate and transparent. as part of a crisis exit strategy, the restrictions must be lifted as soon as possible. however, at the meantime, cross border travel, in particular in healthcare and elderly care, as well as in the agri-food sector, must not be limited. these concerns must be mentioned in our resolution.
in writing. the coronavirus epidemic has hit all countries hard. the worst human suffering has been in belgium, spain, italy, and luxembourg. the extent of the economic damage is not known, but it is expected to be severe in all member states. in a context of crisis for all member states, solidarity between member states remains important. i consider it necessary to issue common eurobonds. these would strengthen the euro's position as a reserve currency. globally, 62% of all reserves are held in us dollars and only 21% in euros. the euro's position as a reserve currency would be significantly strengthened if we could agree on common euro bonds. this would benefit both countries with conservative fiscal policies and those with a high public sector debt burden in the form of lower interest rates. debates on the possibility of issuing common eurobonds began before the euro was born. unfortunately, no agreement was reached on the common eurobonds. as long as the moral hazard of countries with more conservative fiscal policies having to cope with higher interest rates on common bonds persists, there will be no common eurobonds. it is not useful to focus the debate on the question of whether to be for or against eurobonds. we should strive to create common bonds that benefit the whole euro area.
in writing. the joint motion for a resolution on the coordinated action of the union to combat the covid-19 pandemic and its consequences is an initiative jointly tabled by six groups in the european parliament, including the epp group. it looks at the measures taken so far by the european commission and proposes new ones, including ways of reforming the way the union works. my group, the epp, has supported this joint motion for a resolution, because it is time to join forces. the european institutions must rise to the occasion and address the pandemic on the basis of the principles of solidarity and responsibility. with this joint motion for a resolution we are asking the commission to manage the health and social crisis, but also to remember that we must prepare for the recovery of our economy. we must do everything possible to keep our companies afloat, especially smes, to protect jobs and our consumers. the single market is a key tool; economic and social recovery will be accelerated if we keep internal borders open. we therefore call on the european commission to act in coordination with the member states so that the european union can emerge stronger from this crisis.
in writing. – unfortunately, facing the covid-19 pandemic showed that the european commission has not reacted in due time: the measures adopted were not sufficient and its coordination role was lacking. now more than ever we need strong, adequate and timely measures to protect our citizens, our economies including the most impacted sectors, and also the single common market. i welcome the emergency funds and the exceptional measures that allow more flexibility in the use of eu cohesion and structural funding to alleviate the effects of the coronavirus. nevertheless, i am also calling for a more coordinated european action to protect eu citizens and support the economy during and especially after this unprecedented crisis. as many of my colleagues, i call the commission for a common recovery plan within a new, ambitious and long-term budget to mitigate the socio-economic and financial effects of the lockdown. this would prove essential in order to maintain eu’s credibility, solidarity and unity among its member states. this crisis affects everyone and this is crucial that all necessary measures are timely debated and adopted.
in writing. while the health crisis should have been an opportunity to show concrete evidence of european solidarity in action, it is clear that generosity has been shown to the whole world, before our own. on 22 april, the commission unveiled a series of exceptional aid for the union's neighbourhood, which includes the balkan states as well as tunisia and jordan, amounting to eur 3 billion. worse, despite some commendable actions, such as germany's welcome of french patients, the crisis has highlighted the persistent tensions between member states, to the point that even the president of the european commission has had to explain herself. moreover, this tragedy reveals the flaws in the european model of economic development, which the political movement i represent has long denounced. the commission has therefore decided to initiate the procedure laid down in article 93 (2) of the treaty in respect of the application of the principle of equal treatment for men and women. at a time when some voices are being raised in the united states to demand the repatriation of these vital products, how many human tragedies will it take for the union to make its aggiornamento?
in writing. the covid-19 virus, which has spread across the world and in europe, has already tragically killed many thousands of people in europe and around the world, caused irreparable damage and has left over a billion people confined to their homes. this crisis has shown that the european union and its member states are unfortunately not sufficiently solidarity-oriented to concentrate and coordinate their response to this pandemic. the ongoing pandemic and isolation have hit the most vulnerable groups in society, including the elderly, people with disabilities, people who already suffer from poor health, as well as people facing domestic violence, especially women and children. the crisis requires urgent and non-bureaucratic measures to mobilise the financial capacity to ensure the availability of the necessary medical equipment and protective equipment, the large research capacity and the funding for the development of medicines and vaccines. no one knows how long this crisis will last, but its economic and social consequences will be long-lasting and damaging. the commission must therefore present a new multiannual financial perspective, adapted to the current situation, aimed at strengthening social welfare and economic recovery. we need a european child guarantee, a european unemployment insurance scheme, a minimum wage to ensure the safety of the most vulnerable.
in writing. the original aim of the resolution was to express the european parliament's readiness to act as a united front in times of emergency to protect the lives of europeans and to help the european economy recover from the pandemic as soon as possible. unfortunately, the text was watered down by the left-liberal forces and made the subject of an ideological struggle, and only four groups finally gave their names; the plan was thus foiled, seriously weakening the legitimacy of the resolution. moreover, it has been forced to include subjects that have nothing to do with the original objective, such as the relocation of migrants, the european public prosecutor's office or the transition to majority decision-making. the text mentions hungary and poland in a negative context, which are doing particularly well in the management of the coronavirus pandemic. it is unacceptable that the measures taken by the hungarian government are presented as being subject to serious errors of fact. it's also undermining the credibility of the ep. the citizens of the european union expect from the european parliament not petty party politics but powerful, swift and tangible measures that can make a real contribution to overcoming the pandemic and the resulting economic crisis, and that alleviate the delay caused by the eu institutions' misperception of the situation. there is no need for the european union to hide its delayed response by criticising certain member states. it is not a matter of the institutions discrediting individual member states on the pretext of ideological battles.
in writing. – in the context of the current crisis, one of the priorities of the itre committee is to protect europe’s 25 million small and medium enterprises, as well as the sectors of industries most affected, including those sectors that are suffering from disruption of supply chains. accordingly, i reinforce the call for a european horizontal strategy for sme recovery to support them by providing continuous access to finance, reducing red tape and fostering investments in strategic value chains. i express support for the reintegration of supply chains inside the eu and for the proposal for a health autonomy action in strategic areas such as active pharmaceutical ingredients (api). in the same context, i highlight the importance of strengthening the competences, budget and staff of the ecdc and ema, to enable these agencies to efficiently coordinate medical responses during crisis. i am conviced that the eu recovery package will have at its core enough financial resources. finally, the covid-19 crisis proved one more time the necessity to further integrate eu space programs galileo and copernicus. the added value of these systems along with the use of artificial intelligence play a decisive role in combating pandemics and crisis.
in writing. in the last three months, the european parliament has been in the news for three things: for approving the climate emergency, for singing the hand when the uk left and for inviting greta to give a conference in the middle of a pandemic, with the building closed even to state visits. these were the priorities of this parliament until the wuhan coronavirus hit. now the consensus groups are asking us to join a joint motion for a resolution calling for even more powers and more bureaucratic bodies to be given to the european union at the expense of the nations. they haven't learned anything. if this emergency has taught us anything, it is that, in the hour of truth, the institutions that count are the family and the national community. they're the ones who'll get us out of this crisis. we cannot continue to sacrifice these institutions on the altar of political correctness, bureaucracy and green agreements. europe must help, not hinder. we will vote yes to economic aid to alleviate this emergency, or to investigate the role of the chinese dictatorship in the spread of this pandemic, but not to plans to advance the federalist agenda.
in writing. the crisis caused by the covid-19 pandemic goes beyond a health crisis, presents challenges at all levels and the potential impact on all sectors of society is yet to be determined. this crisis affects all countries and knows no borders. it has claimed thousands of lives around the world and millions of households will suffer the long-term consequences. that is why we european socialists demand strong, coordinated action from the eu, with citizens and their fundamental rights at its centre. we call for the protection of the most vulnerable so that no one is left behind and for a recovery with the green deal and digital transformation at its core. we also call on the european commission and the council to provide a 'large-scale recovery and reconstruction package' to support the european economy after the crisis, as well as 'recovery bonds guaranteed by the eu budget'. the eu has to rise to the occasion.
mr president, as we fight to eradicate this pandemic, we are facing the largest economic crisis, probably, in 100 years. coming out of this crisis requires us to mobilise large amounts of public investment. sadly, our member states coming out of a large euro crisis are too burdened with debt to undertake this investment. that’s why the solution is that europe rises to the challenge and europe puts together this plan, this schuman plan, if you allow me to call it this way. this parliament proposes that the european commission raises a two trillion recovery package that is financed by recovery bonds, which allows us to invest in medical, in green deal, in digitalisation, in all the priorities of our european citizens. now, the big question is: how do we finance such a plan? five groups in this parliament are offering a grand bargain to our member states. we are offering them a deal. we are saying: ‘we understand you’re really constrained, we understand it’s hard for you to raise your contributions’. that’s why we think these interests and this principle from these long—term loans that we need to pay for the plan have to be paid with new own resources that make those who cheat on their taxes, the digital companies that are not contributing as much as they should, the big polluters, pay what they should be paying. this plan, madam president of the commission, is economically sound because we need this investment, is politically feasible, as you will see tomorrow when you count the votes. it’s feasible to be ambitious, it’s feasible to have an ambitious plan, and it’s financially possible because our citizens are looking for good investment vehicles into the future. there is demand for such an investment vehicle. seventy years ago, mr robert schuman said ‘we need to make europe in the concrete’. this is the moment to give the concrete steps, but ambitious steps, to build europe forward.
mr president, the crisis has blown up the debate on the multiannual financial perspective, just as we were doing just a few months ago. the budget of the european commission will no longer be just the budget of the european commission, it will also be the resources which will ultimately support the reconstruction fund and the european bonds which have to finance this fund. the budget must therefore make a giant leap of at least 2% of european gdp. the typical and exhausting debates about whether the commission's budget should be a little over or a little under 1% have been ridiculously pulverized by circumstances, and this brings us to an even bigger debate. how could the commission have a budget of this magnitude without new own resources other than the present ones? many members have repeated this in the house today. is it not time to raise taxes of our own? not just own taxes within the framework of own resources, beyond customs duties and the vat rate; taxes consistent with the new european green deal, taxes which will help us to reduce inequalities, indeed, or perhaps by raising at european level some taxes which already work today at state level in some countries. the coronavirus crisis has therefore opened the door wide to a debate that has been too silent until now: the need to build a true european tax system. this parliament must lead this debate. the european council will not do that. it's now or never.
mr president, i would like to thank you for your service. the next mff is crucial for the post-crisis recovery of our economies and needs to be ambitious and of the right size. the commission's recovery fund is also necessary in view of the gravity of the crisis, but it cannot be at the expense of traditional union policies, which have repeatedly proven their usefulness. the cohesion policy and the agricultural policy must therefore be strengthened. cohesion policy is the eu's most important investment tool, from which all member states benefit. maintain the recently introduced flexibility in the use of cohesion support, at least for the first years of the new mff. as the hungarian representative, i will pay particular attention to the fair distribution of funds. hungary has achieved outstanding performance in economic recovery in recent years, with growth of around 5%. we're now also leading the way in managing the epidemic, mobilizing the third largest proportion of resources, creating new jobs for every job lost. i hope that this great work is not detrimental to the eu. please ensure that there is no moral hazard in the allocation of resources, and that neither the mff nor the recovery fund reward irresponsible management or the circumvention of the epidemic. the aim should be to provide assistance to each member state according to its needs and merits.
chairman, after the lockdown, back out. and what's the best way out? there are three choices. first, a recovery for all. the euro crisis teaches us that a problem for one country is a problem for all countries. so don't put off problems until later. it is time for joint loans and mutual aid so that every country can emerge from the crisis. that is why parliament's resolution is so good. secondly, a sustainable recovery that does not focus on profits but on people and the environment. so let's legislate that no investment from the recovery fund should harm people or the environment. and third, the strongest shoulders, the heaviest loads. this calls for an end to tax evasion, including within the european union. this also requires a contribution to the internal market from the largest companies, a contribution to the european union's own resources. that's three choices for the best way out.
mr. chairman, please! i would like to address four points in my speech. firstly, i would like to emphasise very strongly that cohesion policy must continue as it is, because this is the most effective way of balancing the level of development between the individual countries and regions. second, the cap and farmers have ensured food security for europeans during the pandemic. not only must this policy be continued, but it must also be strengthened, including in financial terms, especially as farmers are to be subject to additional obligations. thirdly, the eu's economic reconstruction fund must be a grant rather than a loan, because some countries, particularly in southern europe, are already unable to take on more debt. finally, fourthly, the commission is to be commended for its rapid decision-making on public aid in individual countries, but i would point out that this aid is so varied that it threatens the common market of the union.
mr president, ladies and gentlemen, 70 years ago schuman set the union on its feet after a cruel war that divided us. today the enemy is a virus. at first we reacted late and individually, but today the important thing is that we have learned. we are all here together, breaking down physical and ideological boundaries, passing a resolution that meets what the citizens expect of us: unity and not division. that is the message we are sending to the commission and the council. the pandemic leaves us in mourning for the loss of thousands of loved ones. it leaves millions unemployed, destroying smes, all of which are key to europe's development. we must therefore bear in mind that our resolution benefits and is the responsibility of each and every european. this requires us to be responsible in the use of resources. your destiny is to lead europeans forward and leave no one behind. we must not allow particular political agendas to be implemented. the recovery plan must serve to lift europe up to the new reality, the union of the 21st century. covid-19 has become a transformation accelerator. let us face the reindustrialisation of europe. we will lead the digital transition. we stand for agriculture, tourism, the green economy and the strength of our health systems and our health workers, without ever giving up freedom, equality and solidarity. let us make sure that citizens regain their pride in belonging to a europe which protects them and guarantees their development. if 70 years ago schuman did it, today we must do it too.
in writing. government measures and their increased powers, in the time of the corona crisis, must not be abused by governments. and that's in no eu country, including hungary. it is essential that the eu monitors any attempt by a member state to restrict the freedom of citizens and their fundamental rights. especially in countries that have long sounded the warning signals of non-compliance with democratic rules. parliament must have the power to end the state of emergency. the international agreements remain in force. powers shall be clearly defined and not be deliberately defined to provide the widest possible room for manoeuvre in the exercise of power. i consider particularly dangerous the benevolent and arbitrary criminalization of citizens, or the journalistic community, without the possibility of adequately defending themselves at a specific time.
in writing. – while the parliament has still failed to restore its legitimate functioning, it is now arbitrarily extending the scope of the procedures for which it is supposed to have a mandate. the current functioning of the european parliament is based on a bureau decision with limited scope to urgency procedures. deviation from the order of ordinary plenary sessions is also only possible on the grounds of urgency, ensuring the possibility for the president to convene extraordinary sessions of the parliament. commission proposals on the pandemic, discharge and other decisions related to the budgetary power of the parliament do require an urgency procedure. it should be noted however that the extension of the scope of the urgency procedures beyond these is strictly unlawful and would be even if, provided, but not allowed, the bureau decision on the current functioning and the electronic voting system gave a legitimate mandate for the parliament to operate. fisheries partnership agreement, dactyloscopic data exchange in the united kingdom, just to name a few examples, do not meet the criteria of the urgency procedure hence does not fall within the scope of the operation allegedly provided for by the bureau decision allowing the electronic voting system.

mr president, on this 70th anniversary of the founding of the union - our project - europe is facing its most difficult situation since 1950. the coronavirus pandemic is just the latest in a long list of challenges we've faced over the past decade. from climate change to the new geopolitics, to digitization, to brexit, to the election of trump as president of the united states. it is true that schuman said in his declaration - and it has been widely recalled - that europe would not be made all at once, but through the creation of de facto solidarities. but he also said - and it must be said at the same time - that the ecsc was the first step towards a european federation. i therefore welcome the fact that the commission has now taken the initiative to propose a new regulation on the protection of the environment. so this is not a time for small steps, but for a big step forward. it is time to respond to the triple public health, economic and institutional crisis with three unions: health, financial and political.
in writing. on the 70th anniversary of the founding of the eu, we are facing the most difficult situation since 1950. the coronavirus pandemic leaves us with thousands of deaths and an economic depression unlike any since 1929, adding to the challenges we've faced in the last decade: climate change, aggressive geopolitics, migration crises, digital challenge, brexit and the election of trump in the us, among others. in these seven decades, europe has experienced the longest period of peace and prosperity, with the world's largest and most integrated internal market, the euro and a series of collective investment policies that have guaranteed political freedom, economic progress and social well-being. on 9 may 1950, robert schuman said that europe 'would not be made all at once, but by creating de facto solidarities' but he also said that the ecsc was the first stage 'for a european federation', a historic mission which we must complete. europe must therefore equip itself with new instruments to address the three crises we are experiencing at the moment: public health, economic and political crises that we can only address through a european health, financial and political union.
madam president, ladies and gentlemen, i think it is worth recalling that vaccines are a health solution which is needed for a prevention policy, they are medicines which are used for healthy people, not for the sick, and therefore for the majority of the population. so not only do they have to be effective, but they have to be absolutely safe, have no side effects, and be created and distributed in large quantities throughout the population. their development therefore requires very long periods of research, production and distribution and therefore they are not a solution to the immediate urgency we have at the moment. we are in the middle of a pandemic crisis right now and there are thousands of people dying in the european union. $7.4 billion raised? the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. unfortunately, this is happening with the obstacle, with the resistance of the pharmaceutical industry, which is clearly not interested in this therapeutic solution. president, this is a scandal! we have the solution at our fingertips, we can treat people who are dying and we can save our economy with this solution, but we absolutely must stop the pharmaceutical companies from making their own private profit. we must instead safeguard the general interest.
madam president, colleagues, this may, 40 years ago, the world was freed from smallpox thanks to another global effort of ten years and more than 500 million vaccines. covid is the first crisis of globalization, it has gone around the world in just 115 days and this virus has shown us our fragility. so we need a vaccine and a treatment to give a definitive solution to this pandemic. i want to applaud the commission's leadership in securing over eur 7 billion to fund the development of the covid vaccine. it's the largest public-private, global healthcare collaboration project. it's time to be brave, but also generous. we cannot allow price wars, wars between countries, or wars between the public and the private. we have to do this together and we have to remove any barriers to access to vaccines, which should be available to everyone in every corner of the world. we have paid a heavy price for the relocation of production in a strategic sector such as health care, also in spain. we must focus on reindustrialising health and research in europe, and europe differs from the united states and china in having the best national public and universal health systems in the world. this is our great added value. this crisis therefore provides us with an opportunity to review and strengthen the european health system, to make it more resilient; and the european union must have more health competences to coordinate a single european health system quickly and efficiently. we must work for the european health union.
in writing. the development of new vaccines and therapies for covid-19 disease is critical for europe and also for its most vulnerable partners such as africa, as no country can overcome this pandemic on its own. international cooperation has added value and allows for the breaking down of bottlenecks and the anticipation of possible shortages of medicines and products. their development is being accelerated globally and research and innovation is being supported. we need global coordinated action to achieve innovative actions and to launch a message of unity and solidarity. there are currently eight vaccines in clinical trials in humans, three of which are receiving european funding through horizon 2020. the european medicines agency has also stepped up its response to this pandemic, coordinating a european clinical trial network, providing free scientific advice and pooling data on treatments that, while requiring more trials, have received much publicity such as hydroxychloroquine and remdesivir. moreover, once new treatments and vaccines are developed, we need fast, equitable, fairly priced access that is accessible worldwide, for which the joint procurement mechanism put in place by the eu will be of particular relevance and must be strengthened, involving all responsible parties.
in writing. since the onset of the health crisis, it has become clear that no country can fight the pandemic alone. containment measures have so far proved effective in slowing the spread of the virus, but they will not eradicate it. this requires further coordinated action in three areas: early diagnosis; targeted treatment for those who are sick; and a vaccine to ensure immunisation. the work of states and eu institutions needs to be coordinated to find an effective vaccine and treatment to tackle the coronavirus. we must also involve the private and public sectors. the international donors' conference organised by the commission was a good example, with over €7.4 billion raised. we need to send a strong message about the need for a future vaccine that is universal, free, fair, equitable and fast. at european level, the joint procurement mechanism is the tool to ensure this. finally, the eu must take the lead globally in ensuring universal and free access to the vaccine and future treatments. the vaccine cannot be a new source of inequality, nor should it be used as an instrument of geopolitical competition. we need to cooperate where some want to compete.

mr president, the current pandemic has shown that the mobility of migrant workers in europe does not always respect equal treatment with nationals. i am referring to both intra-european workers and workers from third countries, such as temporary workers in the agricultural sector, who have contributed so much to the supply of basic products during this crisis. we have heard of cases of extreme, precarious working conditions and risks to their health and lives. it is therefore necessary to detect possible abuses and fraud, because, although they are minor, they are very bloody and damage the image of the sector and create unfair competition. it also occurs in other sectors where fraudulent labour brokers operate, who take workers in one country to take them to another in conditions of labour exploitation and unprotected, as was the case with spanish, romanian and bulgarian workers in the netherlands. europe cannot afford this. all workers without exception must enjoy equal treatment, social protection, decent working conditions and access to state aid, regardless of their country of origin or destination.

because, ladies and gentlemen, for our climate strategy to have a real global impact, we need partners to join it, and that can only be achieved on the basis of binding agreements. we must be vigilant in this parliament about the political actions of those governments in the region which are going in the wrong direction, but we must also acknowledge the efforts of the amazonian countries, which have signed the paris agreement, the leticia pact or the escazú agreement and which have developed national initiatives to combat this situation amid tremendous economic and social difficulties. we must also support civil society, indigenous peoples, environmentalists, that is, countries. because countries are not just their governments. they, like us, pass, but the problems persist, and only with instruments of real impact can we contribute to our shared goal. and the most powerful instrument we have is trade and association agreements. these are key to working with our partners on climate strategy, such as the eu-mercosur agreement, which includes a specific chapter on sustainable development and environmental protection, and which includes compliance with the paris agreement as a binding element. ladies and gentlemen, it will not be with ideological demagoguery that we will put out fires or curb illegal mining or deforestation or land grabbing, but with respect, resources, cooperation and agreements.
