mr president, after reading and listening to the commission's communication, we should not only be horrified, we should be ashamed of our inability to assume the responsibilities that fall to us in this matter. europe has become the world centre for the distribution of child pornography. this is the case when 23 of the 27 member states - as has been pointed out - have not transposed the directive on combating sexual abuse. we need to increase the penalties for such behaviour and harmonise the types of penalties. we need to establish a legal framework quickly so that internet service providers can act more effectively in this area. it is essential that we support more and work closely with civil society organisations who are also in this fight. we need new instruments for crossborder judicial cooperation and we need to strengthen the competence of europol, which has proved to be a privileged instrument for its effectiveness in police cooperation. we must declare war on all those who every year destroy the lives of thousands of children.
madam president, i would like to thank you. moria is not an unfortunate development. moria is a political choice made by the commission, together with the member states. it's supposed to discourage, dehumanize and treat people as if they're not human at all. i would like to say here in the european parliament that it is a shame that my country, sweden, has not offered to take in a single person affected by moria. but it is also shameful that the commission is now saying that we are going to build a new camp on lesbos. we're not having any camps. we're closing the camps. we are evacuating moria. we are going to agree on a policy, a model to be presented next week, based on sharing the responsibility for the reception - a dignified reception - where we treat people who come to europe the way we ourselves would like to be treated. it's no more difficult than that, and there can be no camps in it. as a former colleague said: it's the year 2020. this is europe. this continent should have no camps.
madam president, the moria camp was designed to hold 2,800 people. when it burned, 13,000 refugees were living in this camp, the largest in europe, in inhumane conditions, with no drinking water, with rationed food, with a toilet for 150 people and a shower for 500. when the camp was closed because of covid, violence increased and mental health issues exploded. who is responsible for this humanitarian catastrophe? the european union can no longer hide its responsibility, our asylum policy is directly responsible for this shame. what was burned in the camp of moria was our collective dignity. we urgently need a common reception programme, a much more solidary asylum policy which obliges all the member states of the european union to assume their share of responsibility and which takes into account the capacities of regional and local governments. spain wouldn't take one of moria's refugees? without effective defence of human rights, the european union is useless, let us not forget.
madam president, commissioner johansson, the tragedy in moria was perfectly predictable and was announced: 12 000 people without a roof, without medicine... there have been 25 000 women locked up in a camp of misery, and that is completely incompatible with europe, whether in lesbos, lampedusa, argu­gu­ine, the canary islands. shame, shame, schande, shame, shame is the cry of the european citizenry who thinks that if we are not able to close all the 'morias', what should be closed is this european parliament, the european commission, the whole of the european union. but the answer can only be to change the way we look at it, which means changing the policy: from a policy of rejection to a policy of humanity and humanitarianism. in the long term, that requires addressing the root causes, in the middle east, in africa, in the mediterranean, including turkey. in the medium term, the new european pact on immigration and asylum calls for a european rescue and salvage mechanism at sea that does not criminalize saving lives, with binding solidarity and shared responsibility. and legal channels and humanitarian visas. but in the short term, we must demand that the greek authorities respect human rights and we must certainly say no to unwarranted returns. but there is also an urgent need for rehousing, not piecemeal or stale: 400 unaccompanied minors, 1600 before the end of the year. the 12 000 people who are being housed there are in need of mandatory and permanent relocation in all the member states of the european union. only then will it be possible to no more moria, never again.
madam president, the moria camp is a tragedy, but it is a tragedy that it existed. it was burned by those inside, because they wanted to speed up their illegal entry into a continent that cannot accommodate all the other continents, although we have here a number of people representing, with that moral superiority, apparently, those who want to welcome everyone who wants to come. because they don't fit. and see if we have a little respect for the europeans. let us see if we respect the greeks, who are suffering, because their habitat has been destroyed on lesbos and on so many other islands. why don't we go back to the roots? why don't we look at turkey, which has been manipulating these refugees, these illegal immigrants who are piling up there, who have no right to asylum, none of them, who should have been deported and taken to their countries of origin a long time ago, but who are used by turkey? why did you all condemn turkey yesterday and now you are passing a resolution where there is not a single sanction against turkey? why is there not even a sanction against turkey? why don't we go to the source and weep and try to bring all the continents here to europe to destroy europe?
madam president, madam commissioner, if the previous situation was already catastrophic, the burning and destruction of the moria refugee camp makes it necessary to urgently re-examine the situation of the refugees trapped on lesbos. our group has no qualms about saying that we do not want another camp like moria. moriah was not a reception camp. it was a concentration camp, with the suspension of basic human rights, overcrowding and unsanitary conditions, and that is why it is necessary to consider the emergency evacuation of all refugees in moria. in this sense, the relocation of all asylum seekers to safe conditions in other member states of the union is necessary, because it is a responsibility of the union. and these actions must be taken as soon as possible, without waiting for another state of emergency, as was about to happen last tuesday. hundreds of municipalities and regions in the union have expressed their interest in receiving asylum seekers and this requires the activation of a relocation plan, including the creation of the necessary infrastructure. and it is the member states that must show the will, but it is the commission that has to exert positive political pressure to make this possible. it is urgent, it must be done now, and let us hope that action in this direction will open the door to a new european migration policy which regards the right to migrate as a basic human right.
madam president, commissioner johansson, what has happened in moria and what is happening daily on the southern border of europe is well known. and it is a consequence of the ineffectiveness and the shameful role of the european union, which for years has tolerated the confinement of migrants in inhuman conditions. what is the european union waiting for to act? what's the point? what we're crying about here today could have been solved yesterday. that is why the european union and the states should ensure the resettlement of all moria refugees today, introduce permanent and binding resettlement mechanisms without further delay and end the violations of the rights of migrants and asylum seekers. we cannot fall into the cynical attitude of lecturing third countries on justice and human rights when it is obvious that we do not respect them either at our own borders or in several of our member states.
madam president, moria was already hell before it burned. the moria fire is not news, its very existence is: over 13,000 people in a camp with a capacity of 4,000. it's not an accident. it is the direct consequence of the european union's harmful, hostile policies on migration and borders. we continue to export weapons to countries in conflict, we continue to plunder resources and fuel wars from which millions of people are fleeing, and when they arrive - those who arrive - we put them in tents in inhuman conditions and deny them the right to seek asylum. we must immediately relocate each and every one of these people, not just boys and girls, and not just from moria, but also from samos, from chios, from calais and from all the camps that stretch across our territory. we must end the militarization of the borders. we must fight effectively against the mafia, offer legal and safe routes and guarantee a pact on migration and asylum. the president of the commission said yesterday in this house that the european union had challenges ahead. the european union has challenges and obligations today, here, in the present: to respect and to comply with international law. we can't look to the future by watching death pass by. we are accomplices, and today that complicity kills.

replacing the rapporteur for the opinion of the committee on constitutional affairs. madam president, i would like to thank my colleague, mr włodzimierz cimoszewicz, who is unable to be here today and i am therefore speaking in his place. indeed, ladies and gentlemen, nothing characterizes our union more than its values. and so, with this first report from the european commission on the rule of law, we can say that we have entered a new phase - 'slightly, everything can be improved' - a phase that is no longer just the stability and growth pact, the fiscal and budgetary rules and the european semester, but the 'let's wait' phase of the six-monthly or annual cycle of rights. i also welcome the legislative own-initiative report drawn up by parliament, which proposes precisely what the commissioner referred to in his final part of the speech: that interinstitutional agreement which is essential to be able to defend effectively the rights and values of the union. a final point: it is very important that when this rule of law mechanism identifies serious breaches of article 2, infringement proceedings before the court of justice are initiated by the commission and, where appropriate, also budgetary conditionality and the activation of article 7 of the treaty.
madam president, the truth is that in a debate like this one cannot help but ask when it became normal for us to discuss the need for the rule of law in europe. at what point did we accept that two far-right governments impose their agenda and normalize the breach of the charter of fundamental rights of the european union? the fact is that i feel that, at bottom, what we are doing here is agreeing how indifferent we are going to be to the violation of rights in certain states. for example, how indifferent we are going to be to hungary gassing migrants at its border, or how indifferent we are to poland declaring areas free of lgbt people. moreover, how indifferent we are to those who hate us, to women. in short, what is being discussed here is a false choice to access public money. but there's no room for middle positions. human rights are either fulfilled or not fulfilled. and it is our duty to enforce them. the vague formula they put forward in the council makes them, in effect, accomplices.
in writing. article 2 of the treaty sets out the eu's core values and the role of the european commission, which is to ensure that the treaties are complied with. a strong commission is essential for the proper functioning of the union, which cannot be constrained by council blocks. the debate on the rule of law is perhaps of greater importance at the moment because it relates to the issues that will certainly be the most important in the years ahead: the financial framework, own resources and the recovery plan. we do not have to choose between democratic quality and economic recovery, both are possible and that is why we ask for responsibility, against feeding divisions and not taking democracy for granted in the face of the rise of populisms. we consider it important that this message reaches the citizens and that is why we insist that the final recipients of the funds are not harmed. the s&d group has been calling for a rule of law mechanism linked to the budget for a long time and we finally have a proposal to start negotiating with. it is important to reach an agreement as soon as possible so that the programmes can start without delay.

in writing. the presence of women on boards is an initiative of the commission from 2012, which the european parliament supported in 2013. the proposal for a directive has since been blocked in the council, as some countries do not agree with the 40% quota for both genders in management posts. the president of the commission announced, on taking office, the priority of this issue, which, when the directive is adopted, would oblige the presence of that 40% in 2020. almost all european countries are far from this objective (iceland and france are ahead, the european average is 28.7% and spain is 27.4%) and experience shows that self-regulation has not resulted in substantial progress, while legislative developments in some countries have brought about notable changes. the spanish socialist delegation supports this initiative on the grounds of social and economic justice, as it is estimated that the inclusion of women on boards would increase the eu's gdp per capita by between 6.1 and 9.6% by 2050.
madam president, at the european summit president sánchez asked this parliament to work quickly to ensure that the money for the european plans is available from 1 january, and mr michel also told us that today. we share, i think, the urgency of this situation and we assure the council that we will do our part; the negotiating teams are working tirelessly to achieve this. but the council and the member states must also do their part. the council must stop blocking the rule of law mechanism, which is so important in preventing money from being used to infringe citizens' rights. and the council must also work with us to develop transparent and demanding rules, which ensure that european money is spent to transform our economies, for the benefit of all. each country must also play its part. we have nine days left until the deadline to deliver the recovery plans, and they must be coherent, transformative, ambitious plans to rebuild our economies for the benefit of all. mr michel is right when he says that the citizens expect us to act, but they also expect us to invest their money well, and we must work together to do that.
madam president, beyond the economic volume or the percentages of financial allocation to sectoral policies, i would like to stress the importance of the participation of the european regions in the management of the future recovery and resilience mechanism. since the beginning of the covid crisis, most regions and local authorities have been at the forefront of the needs of the population, some of them even managing key competences such as health, education or the social sphere. regional administrations therefore know their communities best and have experience in managing european funds. the inclusion of the regions in the management of the funds would allow us to speed up and carry out more projects, thus giving the european union more credibility. ladies and gentlemen, the management of the crisis has made clear the need for greater coordination, but at the same time for greater multi-level governance. only then will we be able to tackle the multiple consequences of the crisis, which has impacted our regions in different ways. some of the amendments to the commission's text, now under negotiation in the european parliament, already contain this proposal. we hope that they will be adopted and that the council will also take them into consideration. thinking globally to act locally is more necessary than ever.

in writing. the european council of 1 and 2 october focused on a number of themes. the foreign policy debate covered the struggle for democracy in belarus, relations with turkey, the poisoning of alexei navalny and eu-china relations. on the internal policy front, the formulas for strengthening the single market and how to develop a more ambitious industrial policy by making eu industries more competitive on a global scale and boosting digital transformation were discussed. the spanish socialist delegation asks the council to intervene to ensure fair and free elections in belarus. in the case of turkey, it must respect international law by returning to dialogue and cooperation. we stand for strategic autonomy that will enable us to rebuild our industrial and digital independence in the face of future crises, guaranteeing social cohesion, building a single market that meets the objectives of the european green deal and ensuring a just digital transition that leaves no one behind. that is why we need a strengthened budget of the european union for the next seven years and the recovery plan to be available as soon as possible.
member of the commission. – mr president. forests occupy about 45% of the european union’s territory. forestry is a place of work: the wood-based industry employs 2.5 million people; about 500 000 european foresters work directly in the forest. the forest is a place for rest, recreation and contact with nature for almost 500 million european citizens, because everybody has some contact with forests. the gross value added from forestry directly is eur 26.5 billion, and about eur 142 billion from the wood-based sector. this is a very important sector. of course, the forest plays a very important role in the protection of biodiversity, climate protection, water management, air protection, etc. i hope this own initiative report will be adopted by the european parliament, because it will be very helpful and inspiring for the forest strategy being prepared by the commission. this strategy will be published in the first quarter of 2021. the main direction of this strategy is defined in the green deal communication, which mentioned that, building on the 23rd biodiversity strategy, the commission would prepare a new forest strategy covering the whole forest cycle and promoting the many services that forests provide. the biodiversity strategy also mentioned that the eu must increase the quantity, quality and resilience of its forest, notably against fires, droughts, pests and diseases, and other threats likely to increase with climate change. there is a strong link between forestry and agriculture and the farm to fork strategy. agroforestry was listed as a potential eco-scheme – a very important part of the first pillar of the common agricultural policy – and the commission will propose agroforestry as a potential eco-scheme in the recommendation to the member states for their national strategic plans. as president von der leyen stated in her recent state of the union speech, our current levels of consumption of raw materials, energy, water, food and land use are not sustainable. we need to change how we treat nature, how we produce and consume. securing the health and resilience of existing and new forests, including under novel climatic conditions in the future, is crucial for their effective support to all the environmental, social and economic functions and services that forests provide to our society. this is why forests are an integral part of the european green deal and its eu biodiversity strategy 2030, which reiterates that forests are hugely important for biodiversity, climate and water regulation, the provision of food, medicines and materials, carbon sequestration and storage, soil stabilisation and the purification of air and water. they are also a natural home for recreation and learning about nature, while culture and heritage are preserved. with lively rural areas and wealthy rural populations, they offer opportunities for people and the planet. against the wider context that i outlined above, the eu forest strategy will reflect the importance of forests for achieving the different objectives and priorities of the european green deal. it will require a holistic approach carried out for, on the one hand, sustainable forest management that improves both their quantity and quality and, on the other hand, the multifunctional role of forests for the economic sector and communities that depend on them. key objectives of the new eu forest strategy will be effective re- and afforestation, as well as forest preservation and restoration in europe. furthermore, the aim is to help increase absorption of co2, reduce the incidence and extent of forest fires and other climate-related pressures, and promote the circular bioeconomy in full respect of ecological principles favourable to biodiversity. the strategy will need the right balance and synergies between the different socioeconomic interests related to forests and the need to increase our forest protection and restoration efforts to achieve our climate and biodiversity goals. the forest strategy will need to have a strong protection and restoration dimension in strong cooperation with foresters and local communities. in line with the biodiversity strategy, the last remaining primary and old growth forests in the eu need to be protected. an inclusive process has already started, involving member states and all stakeholders, to achieve that. in addition, some parts of the new protected areas under the regular protection regime will probably also need to include forests. the upcoming forest strategy will also include a roadmap for planting at least three billion additional trees – maybe more – in the eu by 2030, in full respect of ecological principles. this was a key commitment in the biodiversity strategy to deliver on green deal objectives and thus contribute to both climate mitigation and adaptation and revert biodiversity loss. in parallel, the eu forest strategy will have to have a strong economic dimension. it has to lay the foundations for innovation and the promotion of new products, and to replace the intensive fossil-based materials and effectively contribute to a new low carbon society. as an example, president von der leyen referred in her state of the union speech to turning the construction sector from a carbon source to a carbon sink by applying organic building materials like wood. moreover, under the cap, the eu will also increasingly have to help to ensure that forestry in the eu, together with agriculture, is socially, economically and environmentally sustainable, supporting and strengthening the benefits that forestry brings to european society and providing sustainable economic rewards for farmers, forest managers and the eu as a whole. overall, let me conclude, the new eu forest strategy will aim to build sustainable forest management and, aligned with relevant eu international commitments, the strategy’s principles and objectives should also help inform eu policy relevant to forests and the forest sector. it is essential to ensure consistency on eu forest-related policies and the action on internal forests. we will take the european parliament’s resolution fully into account when developing the upcoming forest strategy, as well as the recently adopted resolution on the commission’s communication on stepping up action to protect the world’s forests, which we welcome. the commission looks forward to working closely together with the european parliament on the future strategy.
the committee on economic and monetary affairs and industrial policy has tabled a number of amendments to the proposal. mr president, i would like to thank you for your reply. the forest is amazing, and it's good that the forest is hot on the political agenda. it's clear that because it offers us recreation, it gives us hunting opportunities, and it's a great opportunity to conserve biodiversity, to build more climate-smart, and to have reasonable carbon sinks that help us with the big climate challenges. in this case, we have to strike a balance in the future so that none of these factors dominates the others. it's also about continuing to show responsibility and seeing that the forest looks very, very different. parts of europe have been deforesting themselves for centuries or millennia, while in sweden we now plant two new trees for every one cut down. this means that in the last 100 years we have more than doubled our forest mass in sweden, while preserving more of the forest for biodiversity values and also getting more out of the forest materials, which allows us to replace fossil-based materials in both construction and the chemical industry. here we have an opportunity for the future, and we must make sure that we do not put ourselves in the way.
i am speaking on behalf of the ppe group. the commission has already made a number of proposals. forests in europe have many functions: they produce wood, they absorb co2, they emit oxygen, they are recreational areas, they are places of experience, and they protect people from natural forces and from avalanches. the latter is particularly true of mountain and alpine forests and the european union's forestry strategy should also have a special focus on these forests. the already difficult economic situation of the forest and the wood is even more complicated there: the harvest is expensive and the transport routes are often long. nevertheless, these forests should and must be managed. only by regularly rejuvenating themselves can they fulfill this protective function. in addition, these forests are often used to produce high-quality wood and are also important sources of energy. and often, innovative cycles have also emerged, local cycles that help to replace imported oil, gas, concrete, plastic or steel with indigenous wood. in these alpine regions we also have functioning forestry services that already ensure sustainability. we do not need to play the role of great teachers here in brussels and create new rules. instead, we should use our opportunities - the cap, the structural funds, horizon - to promote innovative ideas and projects locally and thus also make local wood use interesting.
i am speaking on behalf of the renew group. the commission has already done so. the concept of sustainability has its roots in forestry as early as 1713. of course, our understanding of this is not the same as it was 300 years ago. we need to adapt management to the changing challenges. climate and nature conservation rightly deserve more attention today. the effects of climate change are making forests more vulnerable to pests and forest fires, for example. through research and practice, we now also know more about the specific factors and ecosystem services of the forest: recreation for people, the importance of healthy soils, humus formation, water reserves available to plants, site-adapted tree species. a one-sided focus on a carbon sink is far too short. i am opposed to making the forests strictly protected areas. forests are not static natural history museums. the cultural landscape is constantly changing. only by active management can we meet the challenges. our forests provide raw materials, they complete the resource cycle, they provide an economic location for rural areas. forestry strategy must respond to these developments and bring the different functions of forests to their full potential. our report has shown balance and is pointing in the right direction.
on behalf of the ecr group. mr president, i fully support mr sarvamaa's report on the forestry strategy, and i do so for two main reasons. firstly, although there are aspects of it which i do not share, i believe that it is a very balanced and constructive text which must form the basis of the future european forestry strategy. and secondly, i support this report because, as rapporteur for the ecr group, i have been able to see firsthand the flexibility of the rapporteur in trying to integrate the main demands of all the groups into the final text. however, despite the rapporteur's concessions to the greens - which i consider excessive and unjustified - they have not been enough to satisfy the insatiable appetite to which the greens and others are accustomed. they want it all or nothing. i am surprised that the groups which talk most about dialogue, consensus and understanding in this house are then the first to boycott all those reports where they do not get 100% of their demands.
on behalf of the s&d group. madam president, this act is a historic milestone. we are looking at the legal basis that will guide us to climate neutrality in 2050, the cornerstone of the european green deal and our fight against climate change. the climate act will force us to review other regulations, to increase our renewables, energy efficiency and grid connection targets - we will do this in the itre commission, which is also progressive - and to improve policies that preserve our biodiversity. it is an opportunity for a committed europe, state by state, with a new clean economy. i would like to thank vice-president timmermans and certainly my socialist group and the rapporteur, mrs jytte guteland, and all the groups who believe that the time has come to act. we're going to set the pace for our planet to slow its warming and meet the paris agreement. this act will demonstrate europe's leadership in the face of the forthcoming international climate summits; others are already beginning to follow our path. the current crisis must not be a brake on ambition. we must demonstrate this with this law, and show thousands and thousands of young europeans who have made their voices heard in the streets that we are with them. their future is europe. one last thought. there is no plausible climate ambition without social justice. we will vote for ambition and realism. we've already shown that the economy can improve as we reduce emissions. let's do it together by supporting whoever it costs the most. there is no green pact without a red heart. we must recover by driving a new clean and innovative economy, which is also fair and supportive. i ask the council, the commission and this parliament not to delay this date any further, not to keep the earth waiting any longer.
i have to say, madam president, yes, if we're going to make a climate bill, we have to make it right. that means that we don't allow gas to get into this bill at any point, that we're going to go with gas. what we have to focus on is renewable energy, and we have to do it consistently. the commission has already taken a number of measures to improve the situation in the community. and let's be honest, gas is about as climate friendly as, say, cola light is healthy. methane emissions, co2 emissions -- that's gas, and coke is a risk factor for diabetes. i therefore urge you to vote against every single amendment which relies on gas and tries to let it in through the back door. let's be clear: we need to take bold, decisive action to protect the climate. we need real energy efficiency practices in our homes, in all our buildings. we need strong infrastructure for renewables, and above all we need to fight energy poverty. what we also need to do is to stop talking about technology neutrality so that fossil fuels are not favored any more. one thing is certain, and we can see this in this law: the market does not decide everything on its own, and certainly not when it comes to climate neutrality.
madam president, ladies and gentlemen, we are disappointed by a european union which, when it comes to assessing the rule of law, is silent about the political prisoners of the spanish state. but there is also the union which gives us hope, and the european law on climate change is the best example of this. it is perhaps the most important piece of legislation of the entire term of office, one of the most far-reaching that this parliament has ever voted on. we welcome the fact that it incorporates some of our amendments. the climate neutral union by 2050 and we want to achieve this without jeopardising the competitiveness of our companies or the cohesion of our societies. on the contrary, this historic transition must help us to build an economy that is not only greener but also more equal and more efficient. let us make the new green deal an opportunity to transform european society so that it becomes a more just society. and we 're running out of time . the paris agreement may seem very ambitious if we think about the work that lies ahead. but it's a de minimis goal when you think about it from a scientific point of view. we're late and we have to act immediately!
madam president, the fight against climate change is a task for all of us. we support the efforts and leadership of the european union to undertake a transformation towards a more circular, cleaner, responsible economy that takes care of our health and takes care of our planet. the european union must take the lead in the fight against climate change, but without losing sight of the competitiveness of businesses and job creation. many people are involved in this transition: workers, entrepreneurs, selfemployed, farmers, fishermen, transport operators, young people, all families. all the measures we take must be taken with all the actors, through dialogue, seeking social acceptance and support. we have been talking for a long time about strengthening industrial policy. today there are two thousand workers on strike in spain for the closure of the intensive aluminum production plant in galicia. one of the reasons for the closure has to do with energy costs. the european industry cannot continue to bleed, especially when it is exposed to unfair competition from third countries. we therefore need to accelerate the clean economy, digitalisation, but we also need to strengthen our industrial base and our strategic autonomy, so that the weight of industry reaches 20% of the total national wealth of the member states: this is compatible. we must take concrete action now, be consistent in setting new climate targets, and we cannot pretend that the covid-19 pandemic and brexit don't exist. in these times, especially difficult, let us leave no one behind.
madam president, when we hear about science, we always think, coming from the left, that it is ideology, and that is what happens - we are seeing it in spain continuously - how they protect themselves behind the so-called science, to do barbarities, all the possible arbitrariness with ideology. the debate on the european climate law is already showing clearly the weaknesses of a european union that is subject to the dictates of a new and reinforced green ideology or religion. this climate submission in the european union, in the midst of a pandemic, with immense problems, is not going to bring stability or legal certainty or a long-term vision based on that scientific evidence. of course, sovereignty is not respected, subsidiarity is not respected, common sense is not respected, and, in the end, above all, the interests of europeans are not respected, which are not taken into account when playing with the emission ceilings; we are talking about a reduction of 55, then 60, then, who knows, maybe 70. that is a lack of respect for the interests of the european peoples. all that is not science, it's pure ideology.
in writing. one of the eu's priority objectives is to combat climate change. the european climate law is therefore of fundamental importance for our future. this law is the cornerstone of the european green deal and will enshrine in european legislation the objective of achieving climate neutrality in the eu by 2050 at the latest. we must support this landmark proposal which will make europe a world leader and a benchmark in achieving climate neutrality. this should also serve to strengthen the competitiveness of european industry and to achieve an economic and digital transition. this act should provide a framework to boost new opportunities and put the eu at the forefront of innovation and clean technologies. to do this, we need to create the right economic incentives and promote investment in innovation. it is our obligation to ensure a just transition with the appropriate support for the regions, sectors and citizens most affected. we must all work together to make this law a reality and make it a european success story for our citizens.
the conflict in the south caucasus has been unresolved for more than three decades, mr president, another conflict in the european union's neighbourhood: armenia and azerbaijan, two of the most militarized countries in the world; nagorno-karabakh, a political disputed enclave, a corridor for gas pipelines from the caspian sea to world markets; russia, the region's largest arms exporter; turkey, which is trying to reposition itself as a key player in the caucasus by destabilizing the region, and this must end. this escalation of conflict and aggression has the potential to create thousands of refugees and internally displaced persons. we are clearly supporting the request of the un and the high representative and are calling for an immediate ceasefire, an end to hostilities, and a return to the negotiating table within the framework of the minsk group without preconditions, as armenia has already declared that it is prepared to do. the european union must be involved, in a coordinated way, in collaboration with regional bodies, civil society and neighbouring countries. after 17 years of neighbourhood policy we have more conflict on our borders. european countries are divided on their foreign policy. the european union, on the outside, is increasingly irrelevant. we need a new neighbourhood policy, cooperative and not competitive, based on the principles of human security, of the populations, a culture of peace and disarmament, with economic development always to the mutual benefit. if not, we will continue to suffer the consequences.
mr president, mr borrell was referring to the enormous amount of propaganda surrounding the conflict and the difficulty in establishing the facts. so a good analysis is surely appropriate. this is certainly not a religious war, as has sometimes been suggested, creating a crusade, nor do i think it is particularly useful to talk about the mass ethnic killings of a century ago. it's a conflict that grew out of the soviet union and its dissolution, and it's a demonstration that what we call frozen conflicts, in this climate of global geopolitical warming, can thaw and create conflict immediately. the participation of turkey. a first effect of this debate is that almost no one talks about azerbaijan, baku or aliyev; it seems to be a conflict between turkey and armenia and we leave out one of the main actors. the main issue of turkey's participation, even if participation could not be confirmed with the military deployment, is the bellicose rhetoric. turkey is the only country in the world that has joined the war rhetoric and not the universal chorus of voices that is calling for a ceasefire, de-escalation and the resumption of negotiations. that is our role, mr borrell: in this new climate with turkey, to demand that turkey join the international community in this position.
in writing. the azerbaijani invasion of nagorno-karabakh has already claimed several hundred lives, including civilians. churches and cultural monuments have been destroyed. the international community, including the european union, should not stand by but must actively engage to end the fighting and investigate possible crimes. violence is never the answer, it can only be found at the negotiating table.
the next item is the statement by the vice-president of the commission, the high representative of the union for foreign affairs and security policy, on the eu's diplomatic mission to venezuela in view of the possible elections (2020/2810 ((rsp)). the procedure for blue cards and spontaneous speeches is the same as in the other debates.
vice-president of the commission / high representative of the union for foreign affairs and security policy. – mr president, i think i can speak in spanish because i believe that the majority of members who are going to follow this debate speak spanish very well. so, if you don’t mind, i will use the spanish language, which, after all, is the second most spoken language in the world. señor presidente, señoras y señores diputados, este pleno ha abordado ya muchas veces las diversas crisis que golpean a venezuela. y el paulatino colapso de lo que una vez fue uno de los países más ricos del mundo occidental, del hemisferio occidental, es hoy una de las grandes tragedias de nuestra época, por lo que no puedo sino compartir su interés y su preocupación por la situación en la que se encuentra el pueblo de venezuela. y no solo venezuela, sino toda la región, que se ve afectada por el número extraordinario de personas que han dejado ese país. conocemos de sobra la situación en venezuela: cómo la sociedad venezolana se encuentra profundamente fracturada, también políticamente. ni siquiera la propia oposición tiene la unidad necesaria para hacer frente a esta dramática situación; una situación que nos han descrito bien los recientes informes de la misión de investigación y, sobre todo, de la oficina del alto comisionado de las naciones unidas para los derechos humanos. y es obvio decir ⸺lo hemos dicho muchas veces⸺ que condenamos la violación de los derechos humanos en venezuela y por esta razón es por la que hemos estado trabajando con la sociedad civil de ese país desde hace mucho tiempo y en particular durante este verano. no voy a detenerme en explicar cuál es la situación política en venezuela —la conocen ustedes de sobra—, pero quiero centrar mi intervención en el título de la interpelación y explicarles el desarrollo de los acontecimientos desde el pasado mes de julio, cuando empezamos una tarea, en coordinación con los estados miembros de la unión europea y con el apoyo de los socios latinoamericanos del grupo internacional de contacto, para tratar de ayudar a encontrar una solución democrática y pacífica a la situación por la que pasa venezuela. y el primer paso para intentar ⸺intentar, digo bien, intentar⸺ salir del punto muerto político en el que se encuentra venezuela era promover el marco para unas elecciones legislativas creíbles, en el caso de que tal cosa fuera posible. pero créanme, no me he lanzado al ruedo como un espontáneo, armado con mi simple muletilla. empecé a hacerlo porque así me lo pidió el g4, dirigido por el presidente guaidó, para que trasladase al régimen las condiciones mínimas que la oposición consideraba necesarias para poder participar en un proceso electoral creíble. así lo hice y, durante todo el mes de agosto, durante todo el verano, he mantenido contactos por videoconferencia, telefónicos, tanto con el g4 como con otros líderes de la oposición, como, por supuesto, con el gobierno del señor maduro, y de todo ello he hecho declaraciones públicas, tanto en foros formales como en la reunión ministerial del grupo internacional de contacto, que tuvo lugar a la vuelta del verano el 17 de septiembre, y en el consejo de asuntos exteriores del 21 de septiembre. durante el mes de agosto emití comunicados explicando que no se daban las condiciones para que se pudieran considerar como libres y participativas las elecciones convocadas para el 6 de diciembre, pero, al mismo tiempo, insistí tanto al gobierno como a la oposición en que debían intentar encontrar una aproximación de sus posiciones para hacer posibles unas elecciones en las que la unión europea pudiese participar como observador. toda esta información es pública y lo ha sido siempre. a la vuelta del verano, uno de los líderes de la oposición, el señor capriles, manifestó su intención de participar en las elecciones si se daban las condiciones para ello, al mismo tiempo que otros, empezando por el propio presidente guaidó, ya consideraban imposible que eso pudiera ocurrir, pero no estaban en contra de que se intentase prolongar el plazo electoral para poder seguir negociando y para poder seguir considerando la posibilidad de una observación electoral por nuestra parte, cosa que solamente podía ocurrir, como ustedes saben muy bien, si previamente se daban las condiciones necesarias para ello. en la reunión del grupo de contacto y en el consejo de asuntos exteriores tuve el mayor apoyo para continuar los contactos con el gobierno para ver si era posible acordar una prolongación del plazo electoral, como condición imprescindible para cualquier otra cosa, porque, como ustedes saben, una misión de observación electoral pasa primero por enviar una misión de exploración electoral, que vuelve con un informe sobre si se dan o no las condiciones necesarias para poder enviar una misión de observación. la decisión de mandar la misión de observación va precedida por una misión de exploración que analiza la situación sobre el terreno, y eso lleva un mínimo de cinco o seis meses y, por lo tanto, cualquier intento era incompatible con la fecha del día 6 de diciembre. había pues que explorar la posibilidad, con lo que todo el mundo estuvo de acuerdo, de que esas elecciones se pospusieran. y, para acelerar los contactos, que ⸺insisto⸺ se han venido desarrollando por teléfono y videoconferencia durante tres meses, decidí enviar a dos funcionarios de nuestro servicio de acción exterior para que sobre el terreno contactaran con todos los actores políticos y vieran cuál era el estado de la situación. eso ha creado una cierta extrañeza en algunas de sus señorías, como si fuera algo novedoso. pero esto es una práctica diplomática totalmente habitual. durante el pasado año hubo tres misiones del grupo internacional de contacto con diversos formatos, todas ellas dirigidas por un alto funcionario de la unión europea y con el mismo objetivo que esta última. el gobierno noruego, con el que hemos colaborado, también ha enviado varias misiones para intentar desbloquear el estancamiento político, la última también durante el verano pasado. esta misión, antes de salir, informó por escrito a los estados miembros de la unión europea, de manera que eso de llamarla clandestina..., vamos, que yo sepa las actividades clandestinas no se comunican por escrito a todos los estados miembros antes de que tengan lugar. y en venezuela estos dos altos funcionarios se reunieron con toda una amplia gama de representantes de sectores. hablaron y escucharon a más de setenta personas. para ser secreta, no ha habido ningún problema en difundir su actividad todo lo que ha sido necesario. por supuesto, se reunieron con el presidente de la asamblea nacional, el señor guaidó, presidente encargado del país, que apoyó los esfuerzos para contribuir a lograr, a intentar lograr, un aplazamiento electoral, siendo muy escéptico al respecto. se reunieron con todas las fuerzas de la oposición, empezando por el g4. los representantes de este grupo insistieron en las condiciones necesarias para unas elecciones democráticas y transparentes, que me adelantaron y que yo mismo transmití al gobierno durante mis contactos en julio y agosto. y además mantuvieron conversaciones con el antiguo jefe de gabinete del señor guaidó, el señor roberto marrero, recientemente liberado de prisión y que lamentablemente no ha sido autorizado a venir a españa, como era su deseo. también se reunieron con don henrique capriles y dos de sus colegas. instaron a la unión europea a mantener abiertos todos los canales de comunicación y aprovechar esta oportunidad para evitar, intentar evitar, que venezuela vaya todavía más hacia un caos político y social que la asola. tuvieron la ocasión de entrevistarse con dos representantes del régimen de maduro, el ministro rodríguez y el gobernador del estado de miranda, héctor rodríguez, en respuesta a la invitación extendida por el régimen a la unión europea para observar las elecciones de diciembre. sí, tuvimos una petición concreta; es la primera vez que se pide a la unión europea que observe las elecciones. primero, pidieron una misión de acompañamiento electoral. les explicamos que tal cosa no existía, que la unión europea tiene unos procedimientos reglados, establecidos, que no acompaña, que observa. y eso, como les he dicho, pasa por un proceso muy pautado de, primero, una exploración y, después, en su caso, la observación. les explicamos que si querían estudiar las posibilidades de una misión de observación electoral había que pasar por este proceso y que eso, inevitablemente, conllevaba un atraso, porque, de lo contrario, no teníamos nada de lo que hablar. los funcionarios allí desplazados no estaban para negociar, sino simplemente para transmitir este importante mensaje a todos los sectores políticos y recibir su respuesta. también se mantuvieron contactos con representantes de la llamada mesa de diálogo nacional y con henri falcón. fue el único grupo en contra del aplazamiento de las elecciones. mantuvieron reuniones con la conferencia episcopal venezolana, que desempeña un papel clave en la sociedad, y que ya saben que ha pedido que la oposición participe en el proceso electoral y en la provisión de asistencia al pueblo venezolano ante la actual crisis humanitaria. los representantes de la conferencia episcopal, tal como habían declarado mediante carta publicada hacía algunas semanas, alertaron de que, si se dejaba pasar la oportunidad de presentarse a estas elecciones, se dejaría el espacio político vacante y el control absoluto de todas las instituciones al régimen de maduro. se reunieron con interlocutores de la sociedad civil, del mundo académico y del mundo empresarial. y creo que en caracas hubo un amplio apoyo a la labor de esa misión y a los esfuerzos de la unión europea para ayudar a lograr el espacio necesario para el diálogo, que sigue siendo nuestro objetivo. porque la unión europea es considerada por todos los interlocutores, o por la mayoría de ellos, como el único actor que puede hablar con todas las facciones y que puede hacerlo con credibilidad. y eso es lo que pretendemos los que defendemos una unión con una dimensión geopolítica. el otro mensaje claro que recibimos es que la gran mayoría de la oposición apoyaría un aplazamiento de las elecciones para dejar tiempo para mejorar las condiciones en que estas pudiesen celebrarse, lo cual quizá nos hubiera permitido enviar una misión de observación electoral. pero la opinión predominante es que, aunque claramente la decisión de aplazar las elecciones por sí sola no es una solución, sería una manera de mantener la puerta abierta a la negociación política. sin embargo, como ustedes saben, el gobierno, después de esa misión, ha contestado de una manera clara y rotunda, diciendo que de ninguna manera van a posponer las elecciones porque la constitución de venezuela se lo impide, ya que hay un mandato muy claro de que se deben hacer en una fecha determinada, antes de que acabe este año, lo cual, obviamente, cierra cualquier posibilidad a que el diálogo continúe en la perspectiva que antes les he explicado. creemos que la única manera de salir del impasse en el que se encuentra venezuela será a través del diálogo y la negociación política entre las fuerzas venezolanas. no habrá un único acontecimiento catártico que haga desaparecer las múltiples crisis que azotan al país. y nuestro papel, nuestra voluntad, es ayudar a encontrar una vía que responda a las necesidades y a las propias demandas del pueblo venezolano. y, después de lo que ha ocurrido y he escuchado a unos y a otros, y, en particular, después de haber recibido, no como respuesta a nuestras cartas, que respondían a la suyas, sino como declaraciones públicas, el mensaje de que las elecciones no se van a posponer, la unión europea, evidentemente, no podrá ni siquiera plantearse, ni siquiera plantearse, el envío de la misión de observación electoral. eso requeriría no solo el respeto a los estándares democráticos, que —como ya he declarado en varias ocasiones— no se dan o no se daban y siguen sin darse, puesto que no ha sido posible modificarlos, sino también, como he explicado, un calendario realista. creo que el comunicado de prensa del régimen de maduro, que se niega a aplazar las elecciones argumentando que no puede hacerlo por mandato constitucional, solo sirve para empeorar la situación política en venezuela. seguiremos apoyando todos los esfuerzos en pro de una salida política, pacífica y democrática de la crisis que el pueblo venezolano sufre, para que pueda superarla como merece.

on behalf of the renew group. – madam president, the tale of wirecard is a tale of economic nationalism. it’s a case of economic nationalism because, when the financial times discovers this scandal, the reaction of the regulators is first to investigate the journalists; second, forbid shorting, forbid trading against the stock, something that has never been done for individual stock in germany; third, continue allowing staff to trade, basically closing your eyes and defending the suspect illicit activity. the problem with economic nationalism is that the supervisor doesn’t have the incentive to act against the companies of their own country. they do it out of national pride. they do it out of economic interest. these are the national champions. wirecard was the leading fintech. there have been many instances before of such economic nationalism. very recently, we had the danske bank scandal where the supervisors in denmark and in estonia of this danish subsidiary see hundreds of billions of euro money laundering and do nothing. we had it during the banking crisis. during the banking crisis, we systematically saw regulators in their home country pretending that the banks were much better than they were. the truth of the matter is that the banks were not good, the supervisors pretended that they were good. what’s the cure against economic nationalism? we know it. we’ve done it in banking. we know the national banking authorities don’t have the incentive to look carefully at their own banks. that’s why we have a single banking supervisor. mr roth, when you say that the solution is not european supervision, i think that this is denying the reality of what we see. the reality of what we see is that, as long as we have national regulators overseeing their national champions, they are going to have the incentive to turn a blind eye. we need common supervision at a european level and to stop with this coordination.
the european commission has set itself the objective of making the project of the capital markets union a reality in 2015 with its first action plan. five years later, he has just presented us with a new action plan. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. this is the great unfinished business of the european integration process, but now, with the severe social and economic crisis resulting from covid-19, it becomes an even greater need for european citizens. why is the union of capital markets within the union important? it is important because it allows small and medium-sized enterprises to have a greater choice of access to capital markets and not have to rely so much on debt. it's also important because it allows small savers, which are nothing but small families, household economies, to have greater investment options. it is important that there should be a capital markets union so that there are no obstacles, so that there are no obstacles, so that the taxation on these capital movements is reduced, but above all it is important that europe becomes a more competitive market, is able to attract more capital within these markets, is able not only to allow european companies to find financing options within the market, but also to allow those who are looking for financing in the global world, in other markets, to see us as a competitive market, an attractive market. and it's even more important in light of brexit. in all these circumstances we have worked on a report which, in seven blocks, gives suggestions to the commission as to the most important, most urgent tasks for making this capital markets union a reality. we have worked, and i want to take this opportunity to thank all the alternative rapporteurs and all the teams who have worked on this time-tested report, for their work, so that we can at least get it approved in the econ committee before the commission publishes its action plan. and we did it. today we can make an assessment of that action plan, which was not in the original report, and in that assessment, my personal assessment is that there is still a lot to be done and we cannot waste any more time. the commission is based on the recommendations of the high level forum and is able to identify the steps to be taken, but it does not set objectives, it does not set deadlines, it is not even able to give solutions to the most complicated issues, to the issues where there is a lack of consensus. the capital markets union is a very necessary and very important tool in the face of the great economic crisis and economic recession which the citizens of europe are going to suffer. i hope that the commission will be able to work with the member states and also with the main players in the financial markets to put this reality into motion. we need to be able to have a europe where smes can find financing on the capital markets, where small savers have more and more investment options and in a safer way. we need a europe that attracts foreign capital and attracts foreign companies to seek capital in our markets. and we need a europe where we work on giving financial education to our citizens, a europe that allows citizens to have the tools to have the capacity to make the right decisions in relation to their investments. so this is a very important project. we welcome the commission's proposal, but we ask for concrete deadlines, concrete measures and for this to be a reality as soon as possible. let us not forget that in europe there is free movement of capital. how can this freedom be a reality if there is no single capital market?
the rapporteur. madam president, thank you very much, vice-president, for being here with us today, and thank you very much to all of you for your contributions to the debate we have had. indeed, as i said in my opening remarks, after this terrible pandemic that we are still suffering, there is a terrible social and economic crisis, and there is no better social policy than job creation. the job creators are the future entrepreneurs, those small start-ups seeking financing to launch a business idea, or the small and mediumsized enterprises, which, as has also been said, are the main job creators within the union. so we need both those who are looking for funding to start a business, and those who are looking for funding to be able to run their business, to have better and greater access to finance. and the capital markets union, the ability to access capital markets throughout the union in an accessible, simplified, barrier-free, tax-free way and in a way that allows these companies to access those funds, will allow for a form of financing that, at this time when we are going into a terrible economic crisis, certainly has to be a reality. but for those funds to exist, we need money to enter the markets, and for that, small savers are key. small savers have to find these capital markets attractive and therefore it has to be done in a safe and attractive way for them, so that they have a more varied investment offer. but nothing in the report i have had the honour of presenting, and nothing in the action plan the commission has presented to us, which is certainly a good action plan, makes sense unless the most complicated issues, namely insolvency, taxation and supervision, are brought to the table, and unless a plan with specific dates, specific objectives and working together with the member states and the rest of the financial sector is put on the table. i therefore ask the commission for more ambition in order to make the capital markets union a reality and i ask you to support the report to be voted on this afternoon by voting in favour of it.

madam president, again one more scandal, again i have to hear my colleagues on both sides of the aisle complaining, and again we see that nothing happens in the fight against money laundering. i think that the key to the answer is that europe needs to be more confident in this fight. we have shown that we can be regulatory leaders. we have put the gdpr standard, and some people would have been afraid that, with this standard, facebook or some others would not operate in europe. the fact of the matter is that, whatever the threats, they cannot afford not to operate in europe. this is similar. if we say to world companies, to companies based abroad, you can’t operate here unless we know who is your owner, if we rule out beneficial ownership, it is not going to be the case that people are going to renounce a 400 million market. they are going to want to be here, they are going to want to serve european citizens. so if we are confident and we set out the regulatory standards for transparency, for disclosure, for this beneficial ownership, it is going to be the case that companies will have to comply. what are the main things we need to change in the way this fight is going? in writing. the scandal of the fincen files revealed by an investigation by the international consortium of investigative journalists has once again highlighted the many gaps in european legislation and supervision in the fight against money laundering. the s&d group has long been at the forefront of pushing for a european framework to combat money laundering and the financing of terrorism. the fincen revelations are here to vindicate our claims. we are calling now, more than ever, for existing legislation to go beyond the existing one, which is clearly insufficient to prevent and combat criminal activity which is increasingly complex and cross-border. we therefore support the move to eu legislation that sets standards beyond the current minimums to ensure a more prescriptive set of rules, that is effectively enforced by member states and that prevents and penalizes bad behaviour by financial institutions.

vice-president of the commission. – madam president, honourable members, the european union has been following the case of dawit isaak from the outset and remains concerned about his whereabouts and health. we have shared these concerns with the authorities in eritrea on numerous occasions. violations of human rights, including the arbitrary detention of political prisoners and non-respect for freedom of speech and the media, are of continued concern to us. we have addressed them and will continue to do so. we have raised these concerns bilaterally and at the appropriate international fora, such as the human rights council, urging the government of eritrea to take concrete steps to address and remedy them. but we can not only criticise. we have to engage proactively to promote positive change. this is not something that happens overnight. it requires continued energetic efforts. i believe that our robust engagement on the political and cooperation track has improved bilateral relations, increased the level of trust and allowed us to discuss sensitive issues with a view to improving respect for human rights. we do that in the interest of improving the living conditions of the people of eritrea and increasing our ability to promote reforms. results, however small, bring us closer to where we would like to be. the bilateral engagement on human rights between the government of eritrea and eamon gilmore, our special representative for human rights; eritrea’s engagement within the human rights council’s universal periodic review; and our support to help them implement some of the recommendations they have accepted are concrete cases in point. the european union looks forward to eritrea’s action plan on the recommendations and is making good progress with the government to start activities soon. i trust that, by engaging in dialogue and exploring ways to work together, we stand a better chance to make our voice heard and to positively influence the country’s human rights record, promote the protection of human rights and fundamental freedoms, and advance the case of dawit isaak.
in writing. the dawit isaak case demonstrates the extreme precariousness of the situation of independent journalists in some countries and the limits of european diplomacy, which has been characterised in this case by sweden's (perhaps too) 'silent' offensive since the early 2000s. indeed, eritrea is still one of the most repressive regimes in the world. in 2015, reporters without borders ranked it last in its press freedom rankings, while religious groups remain under tight government supervision. this oppression is, moreover, permeating the whole of society through a national service which is frequently abused and which is almost like a new form of slavery. every year, it pushes countless citizens of this small african country into the arms of human traffickers and onto global migration routes. in this respect, if europeans and other major powers cannot answer for all the atrocities committed around the world, or even solve them, we should at least refrain from contributing to them, even indirectly, for example by promoting infrastructure projects that could give rise to flagrant violations of fundamental rights.
